<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Some girls join Year $6$ from another school. Half of Year $6$ are now boys. How many girls have joined the school?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of student}     &= 2(BA + BB) \\\\
                                   &= 2(14 + 12) \\\\
                                   &= 52 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of new girls joined}     &= 52 -48 \\\\
                                            &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of student}     &= 2(BA + BB) \\\\
                                   &= 2(14 + 12) \\\\
                                   &= 52 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$

\text{Total number of new girls joined}     &= 52 -48 \\\\
                                            &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Gill has $5$ pink, $7$ purple and $8$ orange counters in a bag. 
If she selects a counter without looking, what is the probability she chooses a pink counter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(pink)}          &= \dfrac {5} {20} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5} {4 \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5}} {4 \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(pink)}          &= \dfrac {5} {20} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5} {4 \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5}} {4 \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {4}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If Gill wants to double her chances of choosing a pink 
counter, how many extra counters should she add to the 
bag? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of extra pink counters $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {5 + a} {20 + a}     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
2 \times (5 + a)            &= 20 + a \\\\
10 + 2a                     &= 20 + a \\\\
2a - a                      &= 20 - 10 \\\\
a                           &= 10
\end {aligned}
$

Extra pink counters $= 10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of extra pink counters $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {5 + a} {20 + a}     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
2 \times (5 + a)            &= 20 + a \\\\
10 + 2a                     &= 20 + a \\\\
2a - a                      &= 20 - 10 \\\\
a                           &= 10
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Extra pink counters $= 10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Here is an octagonal spinner:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


For each statement put a tick $(\checkmark)$ if it is true or a cross $(\text{X})$ if it is false.

$3$ is the most likely score $\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$

$3$ and $4$ are equally likely scores $\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$

Odd and even scores are equally likely $\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$

A score of less than $2$ is more likely than a score of $2$ or more $\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(3)}       &= \dfrac {2} {8} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2} {4 \times 2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {2}} {4 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(4)}       &= \dfrac {2} {8} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2} {4 \times 2} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {2}} {4 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(1)}       &= \dfrac {3} {8}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(Odd)}       &= \dfrac {5} {8}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(even)}       &= \dfrac {3} {8}
\end{aligned}
$

False, True, False, False

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

False, True, False, False

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

Fred has a bag of sweets.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


He is going to take a sweet from the bag at random.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>

<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>

</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the **probability** that Fred will get a **black** sweet?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$\text{P(black sweet)} = \dfrac {1} {20}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {20}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$\text{P(black sweet)} = \dfrac {1} {20}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {20}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write the missing **colour** in the sentence below.

The probability that Fred will get a ...... sweet is $\dfrac{1}{4}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


Let's calculate the probability for various sweets.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(yellow sweet)}  &= \dfrac {3} {20} \\\\
\text{P(green sweet)}   &= \dfrac {5} {20} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5} {4 \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5}} {4 \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
\text{P(purple sweet)}  &= \dfrac {4} {20} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {4} {4 \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {4}} {\cancel {4} \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

Answer = Green sweet

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Green sweet

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
Let's calculate the probability for various sweets.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(yellow sweet)}  &= \dfrac {3} {20} \\\\
\text{P(green sweet)}   &= \dfrac {5} {20} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5} {4 \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5}} {4 \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {4} \\\\
\text{P(purple sweet)}  &= \dfrac {4} {20} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {4} {4 \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {4}} {\cancel {4} \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {5}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Answer = Green sweet
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Green sweet
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the **probability** that Fred will get a **green or a yellow** sweet?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(yellow or green)}   &= \dfrac {3 + 5} {20} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {8} {20} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 4} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel{4}} {5 \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {5}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(yellow or green)}   &= \dfrac {3 + 5} {20} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {8} {20} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 4} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel{4}} {5 \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {2} {5}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question subsubquestion'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$\text {P(six)} = \dfrac {1} {6}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {6}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability

An even number

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Even numbers in a dice $= 2, 4, 6$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {P(even number)}      &= \dfrac {3} {6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3} {2 \times 3} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {3}} {2 \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$\dfrac {1} {2}$

<ul class='subsubquestion lower-roman'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

If I roll the die $60$ times how many times would you expect to get a $2$ or $3$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{P(2 or 3)}       &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\ 
                       &= \dfrac {2} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2}} {3 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\text {Number of times Expected}    &= \text{P(2 or 3)} \times 60 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 60  \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 60} {3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 20} {3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 20} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
                                    &= 20
\end {aligned}
$

It is expected $2$ or $3$ would occur $20$ times.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text{P(2 or 3)}       &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\ 
                       &= \dfrac {2} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2}} {3 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\text {Number of times Expected}    &= \text{P(2 or 3)} \times 60 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 60  \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 60} {3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 20} {3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 20} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
                                    &= 20
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
It is expected $2$ or $3$ would occur $20$ times.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>

If I roll a red die and a blue die together in how many different ways can I get a total of $7$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

If rolling dies together, the possible ways getting a total of $7$ are:

- $1, 6$

In $6$ different ways we can get a total of $7$

<div class='question question'>

An example of an **even** or $50$ - $50$ chance is the probability of throwing a fair coin and getting a head.

Which of the following situations represent an **even** chance ?

Circle the correct answer.

</div>

<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

You obtain a $2$ when rolling a fair six sided dice. EVEN / NOT EVEN

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(2)} &= \dfrac {1} {6}
\end{aligned}
$

Not Even

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Not Even

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>

\end{aligned}

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

you get an even number when rolling a $10$ sided dice. EVEN / NOT EVEN

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Even Numbers between 1 to 10} &= 2,4,6,8,10 \\\\
\text{P(even number)} &= \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\ 
                        &= \dfrac {5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5}} {2 \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {2} 
\end{aligned}
$

Even

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Even

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Even Numbers between 1 to 10} &= 2,4,6,8,10 \\\\
\text{P(even number)} &= \dfrac {5} {10} \\\\ 
                        &= \dfrac {5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {5}} {2 \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {1} {2} 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Even
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Even
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

you get a prime number when rolling a six sided dice. EVEN / NOT EVEN

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Prime numbers between 1 to 6} &= 1,2,3,5 \\\\
\text{P(prime number)}  &= \dfrac {4} {6} \\\\ 
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 2} {2 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {2}} {3 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {3} 
\end{aligned}
$

Not Even

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Not Even

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Prime numbers between 1 to 6} &= 1,2,3,5 \\\\
\text{P(prime number)}  &= \dfrac {4} {6} \\\\ 
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 2} {2 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {2}} {3 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {3} 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Not Even
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Not Even
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
